Path-trace diagnosis of the rolls' lean runaway (shapes plan Task 4; shapes design §4, B1 rulings 5 and 8)

Made with `nice -n 20 npx tsx scripts/pathTrace.ts --diagnose` (practised: practice's last pass) and with
`--diagnose --unpractised` (the first pass, the plan as given), on the full and pass roll text books at their canonical
powers, on the 10 s open lawn, at engine 0.19.0 with the shapes plan's Tasks 1 and 2.

Columns. "off": the actual grip less its planned path (mm, along aim and up). "feed-forward": the hands' planned force.
"rest": everything else the hands apply (flex and chains), F less the feed-forward. "top" and "bottom": each hand's force
on the head (N, along and up). "lever": the bottom hand's moment about the top grip, positive turning the head forward,
which lowers the lean. "balls": the face–ball pairs' normal force, sampled each ms, so the impulses show as spikes.

FINDING: the balls' resistance, which the feed-forward does not include, starts the runaway on both rolls. The hands'
grips do not then arrest the pitch it leaves. On the pass roll a second effect follows: the bottom hand's flex and
chain, loaded by the impulse, spring back from 23 ms to 38 ms and lever the head back, which more than doubles the
lean's rate. On the full roll the bottom hand's levering resists the growth. Practice's lift adds to the runaway but
does not cause it.

- It starts at contact and is present without practice. Played lean, unpractised: full roll 31.0° → 32.4° at 10 ms,
  37.9° at 50 ms, 43.0° at 100 ms (plan 31° → 29°); pass roll 34.0° → 35.1°, 43.3°, 49.6° (plan 34° → 30°).
- The head rotates about the top hand. The top hand stays within about 2 mm of its path for the first 10 ms. The
  bottom grip falls back and rises: at 100 ms it is 36 mm back and 30 mm up (full roll), and 59 mm back and 70 mm up
  (pass roll).
- The balls take the head's momentum at the face: the first contact samples are 603 N at 2 ms and 385 N at 5 ms (full
  roll), and 520 N at 5 ms (pass roll). The feed-forward is a free mallet's, 13–50 N along aim (full roll), and it never
  rises to meet them. The rest of the hands' force rises only after the grips are already off their paths (73 N at 1 ms
  on the full roll, 129 N on the pass roll).
- Full roll: the bottom hand's lever is positive through the first 36 ms (up to 30 N·m), pushing the head forward
  against the growth. Its negative stretch, down to −19 N·m at 48 ms, follows the feed-forward's own planned
  deceleration (−16 N along), with the rest near zero, and the lean's rate, about 0.13°/ms, does not change across
  it. Levering is not the cause on the full roll.
- Pass roll: from 23 ms to 38 ms, after the ball impulses (520 N at 5 ms, 192 N at 20 ms, 160 N at 22 ms), the rest
  turns backward, to −168 N along at 30 ms. It does so while the bottom grip is 34 mm behind its path, so this is the
  flex and the chain springing back, not a correction. The bottom hand pulls back up to −233 N along, a lever of
  −98 N·m. Across it the lean's rate rises from about 0.13°/ms to 0.35°/ms, and it stays near 0.3°/ms to 80 ms
  with no ball force at all. The grips do not take back the pitch rate: the rest pushes forward and down by 10–60 N,
  but the drift goes on.
- Practice makes it worse but did not start it. Practised, the lean at 100 ms is 48.5° (full) and 54.5° (pass),
  against 43.0° and 49.6° unpractised. The lift also adds large vertical feed-forward swings, down to −706 N at 100 ms on
  the full roll.

Discriminating check, for the fix: feed the balls' sensed resistance forward and play both text books again. If the
diagnosis is right, the lean stays within 5° of the plan and the top hand within 10 mm of its path, Task 5's targets,
with HAND_COUPLING and the bottom hand's path unchanged. On the pass roll this should also shrink the bottom hand's
rebound, since the impulse that loads it is met. If the rebound persists, the bottom hand's chain needs its own look.

ADDENDUM, gate 1's check (the same day). The finding above is half wrong: the balls do cause the runaway, but not
because the hands fail to push against them along aim.

Made with `--diagnose --unpractised --instant-push` (the push intent with no rise and no cap: τ_push 1 ns, F_push 1 MN)
and `--diagnose --unpractised --no-balls` (planned at the balls, then played with every ball 3 m on along aim).

                          lean at 100 ms (plan)   top hand off at 100 ms (along, up)   bottom grip off at 100 ms
  full roll, as played     43.0° (29.0°)            +25.7, −14.5 mm                       −35.6, +30.0 mm
  full roll, instant push  42.7°                    +30.1,  −6.2 mm                       −30.1, +37.2 mm
  full roll, no balls      28.9°                     +0.1,  −0.8 mm                        +0.5,  −1.1 mm
  pass roll, as played     49.6° (30.2°)            +34.0,  −7.8 mm                       −59.0, +69.9 mm
  pass roll, instant push  50.8°                    +44.2,  +2.7 mm                       −53.6, +86.1 mm
  pass roll, no balls      30.2°                     +0.2,  −0.8 mm                        +0.3,  −0.8 mm

- Without the balls both rolls follow their plan: the lean stays within 1° of it and both grips within about 5 mm of
  their paths (the pass roll's bottom grip 4.7 mm at 20 ms) through the push. The plan, the feed-forward and the flex
  are sound on their own. The pass roll's bottom-hand pull-back at 30 ms is there without the balls too (−301 N, a
  lever of −127 N·m), yet the lean holds, so it is not a cause: the FINDING's second effect is withdrawn.
- Meeting the balls' resistance along aim at once does not help. The lean is unchanged (full) or worse (pass), and the
  top hand runs further ahead. A push at the hands against a force at the face adds a couple that tips the shaft back.
- So the cause is the balls' moment, not their force. The face–ball impulse acts about 0.83 m below the top hand and
  turns the shaft back about it, at about 2.3 rad/s on the full roll, the 0.13°/ms of the table. Nothing in the push
  holds the shaft's pitch directly, as a stop's hold does (holdPitchGains): the grips resist it only through their
  0.39 m spread, and the bottom grip gives 36–59 mm.
